class ara::diag::DiagnosticSovdContentElementData

[SWS_DM_02307] Upstream: RS_Diag_04260

class DiagnosticSovdContentElementData {...};

Diagnostic SOVD Abstract Content Data.

User GuideAI-GENERATED

Encapsulates typed primitive, array-based, and record-based content for service-oriented vehicle diagnostics.
AI-GENERATED unreviewed Model: gemini-3.8-flash-medium · Effort: medium · Temperature: nicht steuerbar · Prompt: 3044e39ae4fc Provenance

Encapsulates typed primitive, array-based, and record-based content for service-oriented vehicle diagnostics.

The class ara::diag::DiagnosticSovdContentElementData serves as a versatile data container for representing structured SOVD diagnostic content in the AUTOSAR Adaptive Platform [SWS_DM_02307]. It encapsulates primitive types, arrays, and key-based records, which are created and modularly composed via static factory methods. Operations are protected in a type-safe manner via ara::core::Result and return error states in the event of type conflicts or invalid keys [SWS_DM_02317]. All accesses are specified as thread-safe, but require strict compliance with the type-bound interface rules.

Context

The class ara::diag::DiagnosticSovdContentElementData represents data content of SOVD (Service-Oriented Vehicle Diagnostics) elements within the AUTOSAR Adaptive Platform [SWS_DM_02307]. According to ASAM SOVD, diagnostic resources are modeled as structured data consisting of individual or aggregated content elements (AUTOSAR_AP_SWS_Diagnostics, p. 205).

The Diagnostic Management daemon (DM) uses the interface ara::diag::GenericDiagnosticSovdContent to process diagnostic data during method calls such as Read a data resource [SWS_DM_01432] or Write a data resource [SWS_DM_01431], [SWS_DM_02166]. In this context, DiagnosticSovdContentElementData acts as a generic container for encapsulating typed diagnostic content between the DM and the implementing diagnostic applications (AUTOSAR_AP_SWS_Diagnostics, p. 206).

Context diagram: Embedding of DiagnosticSovdContentElementData into the interaction between DM, GenericDiagnosticSovdContent, and diagnostic applications. Sources: [SWS_DM_01431], [SWS_DM_01432], [SWS_DM_02166], AUTOSAR_AP_SWS_Diagnostics, p. 205.

Lifecycle

Objects of DiagnosticSovdContentElementData are instantiated via static factory methods that determine the underlying data variant and content type [SWS_DM_02315]. The class presumably models a variant-based structural pattern (Interpretation: The separate factories and type-bound checks indicate an internal union or tagged-variant structure), in which an instance is either a primitive, an array, or a record.

Typed construction methods are available for creating primitive elements:

  • Integers: CreatePrimitiveContentSignedInt [SWS_DM_02308] and CreatePrimitiveContentUnsignedInt [SWS_DM_02309],
  • Floating-point numbers: CreatePrimitiveContentDouble [SWS_DM_02310],
  • Booleans: CreatePrimitiveContentBool [SWS_DM_02311],
  • Strings: CreatePrimitiveContentString [SWS_DM_02312].

Composite types are created via CreateArrayContentElement [SWS_DM_02313] for sequences or CreateRecordContentElement [SWS_DM_02314] for key-value structures. The destructor manages deallocation and supports move and copy operations [SWS_DM_02316]. While construction and memory-allocating write operations are declared as not exception safe, read accesses and destruction are exception safe, and method calls are thread-safe according to the specification.

Usage Path

A typical flow comprises the creation, incremental population, and subsequent reading of structured data elements. If, for example, a diagnostic record is to be constructed, the application first creates a record instance via CreateRecordContentElement [SWS_DM_02314].

Subsequently, fields with a unique key are registered via overloaded Add methods, such as for primitive integers [SWS_DM_02368], strings [SWS_DM_02372], or nested sub-elements [SWS_DM_02373]. Before read access, the presence of a field can be checked with HasContentElement [SWS_DM_02364] before access is performed via Find [SWS_DM_02365] or the subscript operator [SWS_DM_02357]. For lists, elements are analogously appended via PushBack [SWS_DM_02346], [SWS_DM_02351] and traversed via iterators [SWS_DM_02341], [SWS_DM_02342].

Sequence diagram: Creation, incremental population, and checking of an SOVD record element. Sources: [SWS_DM_02314], [SWS_DM_02364], [SWS_DM_02368].

Error Scenarios

Erroneous operations are indicated via the return type ara::core::Result. Type violations result in errors when type-specific methods are called on non-matching variants—for example, array operations like Capacity [SWS_DM_02336] or Clear [SWS_DM_02338] on record or primitive instances.

Likewise, accesses to nonexistent keys in Find [SWS_DM_02365] or Remove [SWS_DM_02367] fail. For erroneous input values, the error code DiagErrc::kInvalidArgument is returned in specific cases [SWS_DM_02327], [SWS_DM_02337], [SWS_DM_02344]. In addition, the class allows an element to be explicitly marked as faulty: using SetError, an ara::core::ErrorCode is stored [SWS_DM_02318], which can subsequently be verified via HasError [SWS_DM_02317].

This guide is based on the AUTOSAR Adaptive Platform R25-11 specification (diag module) and summarizes the requirements of the document sections and interface declarations.

                                                                                                                                                                                                  Returns the count of elements in the RecordContentElementData. This DiagnosticSovdContentElementData shall be of type RecordContentElementData or ContentData

                                                                                                                                                                                                  use
                                                                                                                                                                                                  

                                                                                                                                                                                                  The synchronous member function RecordCount of class ara::diag::DiagnosticSovdContentElementData determines the number of contained elements of a diagnostic record ([SWS_DM_02353]). The call takes place without parameters and requires that the instance is either of type RecordContentElementData or ContentData (AUTOSAR_AP_SWS_Diagnostics §8.60.6.1.2.49 “RecordCount”).
                                                                                                                                                                                                  

                                                                                                                                                                                                  Error handling
                                                                                                                                                                                                  

                                                                                                                                                                                                  Upon successful query, the function returns a ara::core::Result<std::size_t> with the corresponding counter value ([SWS_DM_02353]). If the object exhibits neither of the two permitted types, the call fails and returns the error code DiagErrc::kInvalidArgument ([SWS_DM_02353]). The error description given in the standard (“rollback_semantics e.g. kPreFailed…”) is presumably likely to represent an editorial table artifact; functionally, the code acts here as a type validation error.
                                                                                                                                                                                                  

                                                                                                                                                                                                  Concurrency and Exception Safety
                                                                                                                                                                                                  

                                                                                                                                                                                                  The method is declared as noexcept and guarantees exception safety (exception safe). In addition, the function is specified as thread-safe, whereby concurrent calls across multiple threads are permitted without additional synchronization ([SWS_DM_02353]).

                                                                                                                                                                                                          auto RecordEnd () noexcept -> ara::core::Result< DiagnosticSovdRecord ContentElementDataIterator >;

    The synchronous member function RecordEnd of class ara::diag::DiagnosticSovdContentElementData provides the end operator for record content data [SWS_DM_02360]. The method belongs to the Diagnostics module and is declared in header ara/diag/diagnostic_sovd_content_element_data.h AUTOSAR_AP_SWS_Diagnostics §8.60.6.1.2.51 “RecordEnd”. It serves to implement the higher-level requirement [RS_Diag_04260].
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Usage and Semantics
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
    The call takes no parameters and is performed synchronously via the signature auto RecordEnd () noexcept -> ara::core::Result<DiagnosticSovdRecordContentElementDataIterator> [SWS_DM_02360]. As a functional precondition, the specification requires that the called object must be of type RecordContentElementData or ContentData [SWS_DM_02360]. Interpretation: The returned iterator is likely intended to act in conjunction with a start operator as a sentinel or termination criterion when sequentially iterating over record fields.
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Error handling
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
    If the instance does not fulfill this type requirement, the function does not return an iterator, but fails with an error [SWS_DM_02360]. In this case, the specific error code DiagErrc::kInvalidArgument is returned in ara::core::Result [SWS_DM_02360].
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Concurrency and guarantees
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
  
                                                                                                                                                                                                          
    With respect to execution safety, the interface is specified as noexcept and exception safe [SWS_DM_02360]. In addition, it is considered thread-safe, which allows concurrent read accesses on the same instance without additional application-side synchronization [SWS_DM_02360].
